# FPS-4 Feature Pipeline Standard, kestrel pump failure model

Owner: reliability analytics. This standard governs how the training data of the kestrel pump failure model is rebuilt for run KP-24. Where a source, a previous run or a habit disagrees with this standard, this standard decides.

## 1. Scope and authority

1.1 Run KP-23 produced wrong training data. Its label export and its settings are kept for audit only. Nothing produced by KP-23 is a reference for KP-24, apart from the one narrow fact named in 2.6.

1.2 The as-of freeze is `2026-05-04T00:00:00Z`. A record whose own decision, approval, recording, receipt or effect instant is after the freeze is carried as written but takes no effect, unless a section below says otherwise. An instant equal to the freeze is not after it.

1.3 The telemetry window runs from `2025-12-25` to `2026-04-30`. Both dates are local calendar days and both are included.

1.4 The source register at the end of this standard lists every file of the estate. A file marked `reference_copy` is a copy kept by another team. It is not authoritative except for the narrow fact 2.6 names for it.

1.5 Rounding is always half to even, applied once at the place this standard names. Means, ratios and differences are computed exactly from the values they read and rounded only when written.

## 2. Export segments and profiles

2.1 Every source except the two profile registers is a sequence of export segments. Each segment carries an identifier, a header or keys, its records, and a control: the record count and the sum of the segment's control field. The control field of a table is its first field of type `q`, `n`, `int`, `ts` or `date`, tried in that order, skipping the record id. The sum skips empty values, takes timestamps as whole epoch seconds, dates as days since 1970-01-01, and quantities and numbers in their canonical unit at their canonical places. Controls describe the shipped values before errata.

2.2 How a segment is written is set by its profile in the export profile registry `profiles_r7.yaml`. For a file and segment, take the registry entries with status `active` and use the one with the highest revision; entries with status `withdrawn` are ignored. An entry with `inherit: draft` takes its dimensions from the entry with the highest revision for the same file and segment in the working draft `profiles_r7_draft.yaml`. Approved errata then correct single dimensions of the chosen entry (2.5); their target record id is `<file>#<segment>#<revision>` of that entry and their field is the dimension name. Segments of the errata register itself are resolved without errata.

2.3 Profile dimensions:

- `cols`: `std` names fields as the field register does, in register order; `alt` names them with the registry's `alt_columns` for the file and writes the record id first, then the other fields in reverse register order.
- `asset_ref`: `id` writes the asset id; `serial` writes the pump serial from the asset's commission or baseline record; `tag` writes the tag that the tag history assigns to the asset for the file's tag system at the record's reference instant (valid_from at or before it and valid_to after it, an empty valid_to being open). The reference instant of a record is its first non-empty timestamp field in register order.
- `site_ref`: `code` writes the site code, `num` the site number of the site register.
- `clock`: `utc` is ISO 8601 with `Z`; `offset` is ISO 8601 with a UTC offset; `epoch` is whole seconds since 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z; `epoch_ms` is milliseconds; `local` is the wall-clock time `<date> HH:MM:SS` written with the segment's `date_form` (the `compact` form writes `YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS`). A local time is read in the time zone of the record's own site field when the record has one, and otherwise in the time zone of the asset's site in force at 12:00 UTC on the written local date.
- `date_form`: `iso` (2026-01-31), `dmy` (31/01/2026), `mdy` (01/31/2026), `compact` (20260131).
- `locale`: `plain` (1234.5), `en` (1,234.5), `de` (1.234,5), `fr` (1 234,5), `ch` (1'234.5). It applies to numbers and integers; epoch values are never grouped.
- `numform`: `decimal` writes the number; `scaled` writes the integer `value x 10^p`, where p is the field's canonical places plus the unit's extra decimal places from the unit register, never below 0, and at least 2 for kelvin.
- `unit_<quantity>`: the unit a quantity is written in. Values are converted to the canonical unit (the first unit of the quantity in the unit register) and rounded to the field's places.
- `nulls`: the token for an empty value of a field marked `?`: `empty` (nothing), `na` (`NA`), `dash` (`-`), `nullword` (`null`, or a JSON null), `n999` (`-999`).
- `status`: `word` writes the code, `caps` the code in capitals, `num` its position in the vocabulary, counting from 1.
- `bool`: `tf` (true or false), `yn` (Y or N), `10` (1 or 0).
- `codeform`: `dec` writes the alarm code in decimal, `pref` as `E-` and four digits, `hex` as `0x` and four hexadecimal digits.

2.4 The canonical text of a value is: timestamps as `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`, dates as ISO dates, quantities and numbers in the canonical unit with exactly the field's places, integers in plain digits, codes as their word, flags as `true` or `false`, asset and site references as asset id and site code, and the empty string for an empty value.

2.5 Errata. An erratum takes effect when its status is `approved` and its approved_at is not after the freeze. For one target file, record id and field the erratum with the latest approved_at wins; on a tie the later row wins. Its value is canonical text (2.4), an empty value meaning empty. It replaces that field in every record of the target file carrying the record id. An erratum naming a record id that does not exist changes nothing. Errata are applied after decoding and never to the errata register.

2.6 Narrow authority of the reference copies:

- `profiles_r7_draft.yaml`: only through `inherit: draft` (2.2).
- `asset_snapshot_q1.xml`: the commissioned date of an asset whose change log has no commission record, taken from its snapshot row with the latest as_of not after the freeze date.
- `calib_certs_pending.html`: a certificate whose status is `see_pending` takes gain, offset and effective_from from the first pending row with the same cert_no, provided that row's approved_at is set and not after the freeze.
- `scada_resend_queue.log`: rows with gapfill set, for a condition interval that has no row at all in the four gateway exports.
- `wo_legacy_mirror.sql`: the closed_at of a work order opened before 2025-03-01 whose effective closed_at is empty, taken from the first mirror row of that work order.
- `alarm_dict_oem_draft.tsv`: alarm codes of an OEM that Appendix A does not list at all for that OEM.
- `ambient_obs_mirror.md`: a site, local day and kind for which `ambient_obs.jsonl` has no observation at all.
- `kp23_labels_export.jsonl`: the review_hold flag of an asset day, nothing else.

## 3. Assets and parameters

3.1 The asset change log is replayed per asset in order of its instant, later rows winning ties; rows after the freeze are ignored. `baseline` and `commission` set the site, setpoint and criticality and make the asset active; `relocate` sets the site; `decommission` makes it inactive and `reinstate` active again; `setpoint` and `criticality` update those values. Before its first row an asset counts as being at the site of that first row. An asset exists when it has a baseline or commission row; its model and serial come from the latest such row.

3.2 The commissioned date is the UTC date of the latest commission row, or the snapshot date of 2.6 when the asset only has a baseline row.

3.3 The rated flow in force at an instant is the flow of the latest performance test row that has both bep and accepted set and whose tested_at is at or before the instant, the later written row winning a tie.

3.4 A run parameter is the run sheet parameter row with status `active` and set_at not after the freeze that has the highest revision, the later row winning a tie. `holdout_assets` lists the assets kept out for testing, separated by spaces.

## 4. Condition telemetry

4.1 Condition intervals come in two groups. Vibration and temperature come from the four gateway exports `cm_gw_*`. Pressure, flow and current come from the three historian exports `hist_a`, `hist_b`, `hist_c` and from the historian backfill, whose rows count only when received_at is not after the freeze. Within a group, one row survives per asset and interval instant: the highest seq, and on equal seq the row read later, reading files in name order and the backfill after the historians, rows in written order. The resend queue is used only as 2.6 allows, its highest seq surviving.

4.2 The sensor on an asset channel at an instant follows the sensor moves of that asset and channel in order of their instant: an install sets the serial, a remove of the current serial clears it.

4.3 The certificate in force for a serial at an instant: certificates recorded not after the freeze, not revoked, with status `approved` (or `see_pending` resolved by 2.6), whose effective_from is at or before the instant; the latest effective_from wins, then the higher revision, then the later row. A certificate is revoked when its latest review not after the freeze (later row on a tie) has outcome `revoke`; a later `withdrawn` review lifts it.

4.4 A raw reading becomes calibrated as gain x raw + offset, rounded to the channel places: vibration 3, temperature 2, pressure 3, flow 2, current 2. A reading without a sensor or certificate in force is unusable.

4.5 A reading inside an accepted fault window of its asset and channel (start at or before it, end after it), decided not after the freeze, is unusable. A usable reading outside the active envelope in force for its asset and channel (latest effective_from at or before the reading, later row on a tie) is quarantined: it is counted as quarantined and is not used. Quarantine is checked last.

4.6 A reading belongs to the local calendar date of its instant in the time zone of the asset's site at that instant.

## 5. Asset days

5.1 An asset day exists for every asset and every day of the telemetry window on which the asset is active at 12:00 UTC. Its site, setpoint and rated flow are those in force at 12:00 UTC on that day.

5.2 Daily values are means of the day's usable readings rounded to the channel places, and the vibration maximum. readings counts usable readings over all channels; quarantined counts quarantined readings. A channel with no usable reading is empty. A reading of zero is a value, not an empty one.

5.3 Availability, first match wins: `embargo` when the effective embargo row (highest revision with set_at not after the freeze, per asset and month) of the day's month is set; `outage` when logged outages cover at least 12 hours of the local day; `standby` when confirmed standby windows cover at least 12 hours of the local day, using for each standby id only its row with the highest sequence; `hold` under 2.6; otherwise `ok`. Coverage is the length of the union of the windows within the day's local midnights. The month of an embargo row is the first day of the month it covers; a row whose month, after errata, is not the first day of a month covers no day.

5.4 Alarm counts. The firmware in force at an alarm is the latest rollout row with status `done` or `rolledback` at or before the alarm; before any such row it is `1.0.0`; its first number is the major version. The class is taken from the Appendix A rows for the OEM of the asset's model and the code whose min_fw is at or below the major version, the row with the highest min_fw winning and, among rows with that same min_fw, the row written first; codes Appendix A does not list for the OEM use the draft dictionary the same way. An alarm counts when its class is `warning` or `trip`, it was not acknowledged as `nuisance` or `test` within 15 minutes (acked_at at or before the alarm plus 15 minutes and not after the freeze), and it is not inside an active shelving window for the same asset and code (start at or before the alarm, end after it). Alarms are counted on the local date of their instant at the asset's site.

## 6. Site days

6.1 Each site has one row per day of the telemetry window. For each shift the row with the highest revision counts, the later row on a tie. A site day is operating when at least two shifts are `operating`.

6.2 Ambient temperature uses the observations whose local date at the site is the day. The latest quality check not after the freeze decides an observation: `reject` drops it, `retest` with a value replaces its value. Per kind the latest observation wins; the mirror applies under 2.6. The day's ambient value is the mean of min and max rounded to 2 places, empty when either is missing.

## 7. Failures and maintenance

7.1 Failure events are the notifications of kind `failure`. Their decisions are the root cause reviews decided not after the freeze and the amendments with status `confirmed` amended not after the freeze, using for each amendment id only its row with the highest sequence. The latest decision instant wins; on a tie an amendment beats a review, then the higher revision or sequence, then the later row. An event is confirmed when that decision is `confirm` or `reclassify`. Its mode is the reclassified mode when one is given, otherwise the notification's mode.

7.2 Deferred amendments: a confirmed amendment amended after the freeze is carried with its event (the latest one when several) and is applied only by the amendment impact of 12.4.

7.3 The failure day is the local date of the failure instant at the asset's site.

7.4 Work orders come from the archive and the current CMMS export. The revision with the highest rev among those revised not after the freeze (then the latest revised_at, then the later row) replaces status and closed_at. 2.6 fills a missing closed_at. A completed work order is a service at the latest labour booking end of that work order, or at its closed_at when it has no booking, and only when that instant is not after the freeze.

7.5 Oil samples count when valid and sampled not after the freeze. Their latest lab check not after the freeze decides them: `reject` drops the sample, `retest` with a value replaces the iron value.

## 8. Samples, labels and splits (phase 2)

8.1 Candidate samples are the asset days from sample_start to sample_end. A candidate is excluded for the first reason that applies: `non_operating` (its site day is missing or not operating), its availability when not `ok`, `low_coverage` (readings below min_readings), `blackout` (a confirmed failure day F of the asset with F at or before the day and the day before F plus blackout_days), `maintenance` (the day equals or follows by one day a service of kind `overhaul` or `impeller`), `purge` (an asset outside the holdout whose day is after train_end minus horizon_days and at or before train_end).

8.2 The split of a kept sample is `test` for holdout assets, otherwise `train` up to train_end, `val` up to val_end and `test` after. The label is 1 when the asset has a confirmed failure day F with the day before F and F at or before the day plus horizon_days, else 0.

## 9. Features (phase 2)

9.1 Windows are calendar days ending on the sample day: 7 days for the `_7d` features and 3 days for `pressure_dev_3d`, using the asset days that exist.

- `vib_mean_7d`: mean of the daily vibration means, at least 4 values.
- `vib_max_7d`: maximum of the daily vibration maxima, at least 4 values.
- `temp_rise_7d`: mean of temperature minus the site day's ambient value, over days that have both, at least 4.
- `pressure_dev_3d`: mean of pressure minus setpoint, at least 2.
- `flow_ratio_7d`: mean of flow divided by rated flow, over days with both and a positive rated flow, at least 4.
- `current_7d`: mean of the daily current, at least 4.
- `days_since_service`: days from the latest service day (local date of a service of kind lubrication, seal, bearing, impeller or overhaul) at or before the sample day.
- `age_days`: days since the commissioned date.
- `iron_ppm`: iron of the latest oil sample whose day is at or before the sample day and less than iron_lookback_days before it, the later instant winning.
- `alarms_7d`: sum of the daily alarm counts, missing days counting 0.

9.2 Decimal features are rounded to 4 places; a feature short of its minimum count, or with no source value, is empty.

## 10. Statistics (phase 2)

10.1 Scaler statistics are fitted on the training samples only, over non-empty values: count, mean, and the sample standard deviation (divisor count minus 1), both rounded to 6 places.

10.2 Class balance per split: rows, positives, negatives and the positive prevalence rounded to 6 places. Class weights come from the training split: rows divided by twice the positives, and rows divided by twice the negatives, rounded to 6 places.

10.3 Bin edges per feature are the training deciles: for k from 1 to 9 the value at position ceil(k x n / 10) of the sorted non-empty training values, counting from 1; none when fewer than 10 values exist.

10.4 The quarantine summary per site counts quarantined readings over all asset days of the site, kept samples, and excluded candidates per reason.

## 11. Training rows (phase 3)

11.1 A standardised value is (feature minus scaler mean) divided by scaler standard deviation using the written statistics, rounded to 4 places; empty when the feature is empty or the deviation is zero.

11.2 The weight of a training sample is class weight x recency x criticality multiplier rounded to 6 places, with recency 0.5 to the power of (days from the sample day to train_end divided by decay_half_life_days) rounded to 6 places first. Per asset, in day order, the weights add up to at most asset_weight_cap: a weight is cut to what remains of the cap, never below zero. Validation and test rows weigh 1.

11.3 The baseline score of a validation or test row is score_bias plus the sum of score_weight times standardised value over its non-empty values, rounded to 6 places. AUC uses average ranks for tied scores and is rounded to 6 places. The threshold is the validation score with the best F1 when rows scoring at or above it are predicted positive, trying scores from highest to lowest and keeping the first best. Precision, recall and F1 on test use that threshold; an empty denominator gives 0.

11.4 Drift per feature compares training and test values against the bin edges: a value goes to the bin numbered by how many edges it exceeds. Bin shares of zero count as 0.0001. PSI is the sum of (test share minus training share) times the natural log of their ratio, rounded to 6 places. Empty shares per split are rounded to 6 places.

## 12. Allocation and amendments (phase 3)

12.1 The review budget is shared across sites in proportion to training positives. The site cap is the whole part of site_cap_share x budget, the region cap the whole part of region_cap_share x budget, using the region of the site register.

12.2 Sites are visited by falling quota (site code ascending on a tie) and each takes the whole part of its quota, limited by the site cap and by what remains of its region cap. Units still left go one at a time to sites with positives by falling fractional remainder (site code on a tie), pass after pass, while caps allow. A site is capped when it received less than the whole part of its quota.

12.3 Quotas are written with 4 places.

12.4 The amendment impact applies every deferred amendment of 7.2 to its event (confirmed when the amendment decides `confirm` or `reclassify`, otherwise unconfirmed) and recomputes the label of every kept sample with the 8.2 rule, keeping samples and splits as they are.

## 13. Lineage

13.1 Every phase 2 and phase 3 file states the upstream digest and the upstream controls of the files its phase read, as the output contract describes.

### Field register

Field types: `rid` record id, `asset` asset reference, `site` site reference, `ts` instant, `date` calendar date, `q:<quantity>:<places>` measured quantity canonical in the first unit of the quantity, `n:<places>` plain number, `int` integer, `code:<vocabulary>` code, `bool` flag, `text` free text, `acode` controller alarm code. A trailing `?` marks a field that may be empty.
